## Adverse Events by Intensity

## OPERETTA 2

There were no Grade 5 AEs in either treatment arm.

Among patients who experienced an AE, the majority of patients had events which were Grade 1 or 2 as the most extreme National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (NCI CTCAE) grade (78.5% vs. 59.8%, OCR arm vs. FINGO arm, respectively).

Grade 3 events were reported less frequently in the OCR arm than the FINGO arm (10.8% vs. 15.2% of patients). With the exception of the events of IRRs under SOC Injury, poisoning and procedural complications in the OCR arm (3.2% vs. 0% of patients) and leukopenia under SOC Blood and lymphatic system disorders in the FINGO arm (0% vs. 2.2% of patients), which are expected events for ocrelizumab and fingolimod respectively, no other clusters of events contributed to an imbalance in Grade 3 events.

There were no Grade 4 AEs in the OCR arm. The proportion of patients in the FINGO arm with Grade 4 AEs was 3.3%:

- Two patients experienced non-serious events of lymphocyte count decreased. For one patient, fingolimod was not interrupted; the event resolved after 58 days. For the other patient, active fingolimod was interrupted. The event resolved after 31 days. Investigators were blinded to laboratory results including lymphocyte counts during the study. As a result, these cases were not classified as serious events.
- One patient experienced serious event of psychotic disorder. The event resolved after 7 days with no interruption to fingolimod.

The most frequent Grade 3-4 AEs by SOC (≥3 patients in either arm) were (OCR arm vs. FINGO arm, respectively):

- Investigations (3.2% vs. 5.4% of patients)
- Infections and infestations (3.2% vs. 2.2% of patients)
- Injury, poisoning and procedural complications (3.2% vs. 2.2% of patients)

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Nervous system disorders (1.1% vs. 3.3% of patients)
- Blood and lymphatic system disorders (0% vs. 3.3% of patients)

## OPERETTA 1

The majority of AEs reported in the study (516/546) were Grade 1 or 2. Grade 3 events (27/546) were experienced by 10 (43.5% [10/23]) patients. Grade 4 events (3/546) were experienced by 2 (8.7% [2/23]) patients.

## Cohort 1:

- 50% of patients (3/6) experienced Grade 3 events.
- o Two events (appendicitis and syncope) were serious.
- o There was one non-serious event of hypernatremia (assessed by the investigator as not related to study drug, and which resolved after 14 days with no treatment or change to study drug).
- One patient (16.7%) experienced serious Grade 4 neutropenia (assessed by the investigator as not related to study drug, and which resolved after 8 days with no treatment or change to study drug).

## Cohort 2:

- 41.1% of patients (7/17) experienced a total of 24 Grade 3 events. These were dominated by 18 events in 2 patients.
- o One patient had 11 events, 8 of which were serious; 2 events were non-serious IRRs; and one event of non-serious anal abscess.
- o Another patient experienced 7 events (all of which were serious, but were all assessed by the investigator as not related to study drug).
- o One patient (5.9%) experienced two Grade 4 events, both of which were serious: alanine aminotransferase increased and blood creatinine phosphokinase increased.

## Serious adverse event/deaths/other significant events

## Deaths

As of the respective CCODs, no deaths were reported in either study.

One death was reported in OPERETTA 1 after the CCOD. A narrative for this patient is provided. The patient died of intoxication and the investigator assessed the event as unrelated to ocrelizumab.

## Serious Adverse Events

## OPERETTA 2

The proportion of patients with at least one SAE in the OCR arm was lower than the FINGO arm (6.5% of patients [6/93] with a total of 7 SAEs vs. 8.7% of patients [8/92] with a total of 18 SAEs). In the OCR arm there were 7 SAEs: infections and infestations (4), psychiatric disorders (1), injury, poisoning and procedural complications (1), nervous system disorders (1). In the FINGO arm, there were 18 SAEs: infections and infestations (4), psychiatric disorders (5), injury, poisoning and procedural complications (2), investigations (2), blood and lymphatic system disorders (2), cardiac disorders (1), general disorders and administration site conditions (1) and Renal and urinary disorders (1).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## OPERETTA 1

As of the CCOD, 26.1% of patients (6/23) experienced at least one SAE, with a total of 21 events reported.

## Cohort 1

33.3% of patients (2/6) experienced a total of 3 SAEs. No action was taken with the study drug in response to these events, and all the events had resolved at the time of reporting.

## Cohort 2

23.5% of patients (4/17) experienced a total of 18 SAEs. All SAEs except for one event of anal abscess were resolved as of the CCOD (anal abscess resolving).

The majority of SAEs (16/18 SAEs) were reported with most extreme intensity of Grade 3, reported in the same patient; no modification or interruption to study drug was required. Four of these Grade 3 SAEs were assessed by the investigator as related to study drug. One patient experienced 2 SAEs of Grade 4 intensity alanine aminotransferase increased and blood creatinine phosphokinase increased; both events resulted in study drug delay (reported as study drug interruption).

## Adverse Events of Special Interest

For both OPERETTA 1 and OPERETTA 2, adverse events of special interest were defined as:

- Cases of potential drug-induced liver injury that include an elevated ALT or AST in combination with either an elevated bilirubin or clinical jaundice, as defined by Hy's Law
- Suspected transmission of an infectious agent by the study drug

No adverse events of special interest were reported in either study, as of the respective CCODs.

## Selected Adverse Events

## Infusion-Related Reactions

## OPERETTA 2

IRRs were observed in both treatment arms; the events observed in the FINGO arm arose from the dummy infusions of ocrelizumab placebo.

## Frequency

The proportion of patients with at least one IRR during the DBP was higher in the OCR arm (48.4% of patients with 78 events) than the FINGO arm (23.9% of patients with 36 events), and the proportion decreased over time for both treatment arms. The incidence of IRRs per 100PY were 57.03 (95% CI: 45.08, 71.18) in the OCR arm and 27.67 (95% CI: 19.38, 38.3) in the FINGO arm.

## Intensity

In the OCR arm, mild or moderate IRRs (Grade 1 or 2) were a common side effect (45.2% of patients). A small number of patients experienced severe events; these occurred during the first two doses. Serious IRRs were rare (occurring in one patient, at the first dose), with symptoms of itching, coughing, flushing and throat irritation; however, the event resolved after 1 day and did not result in change or delay to ocrelizumab dosing. There were no IRRs of Grade 4 or 5.

## Outcome

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

IRRs in the OCR arm typically resolved quickly (68/78 events within 1 or 2 days). Events which lasted longer (up to a week) were mild or moderate, and generally patients received treatment for the reactions.

## Symptoms of IRRs

The most common symptoms in OCR arm were headache, throat irritation, pruritus, rash, erythema and nausea. Grade 3 symptoms were asthenia and rash; dysphonia and oropharyngeal discomfort; cough, throat irritation, pruritus, and flushing; all occurring in 1.1%).

## Timing

IRRs were most likely to occur at the start of ocrelizumab treatment, with 31.2% of patients having a reaction on Day 1 of Dose 1. This proportion dropped to 8.6% for the second part of Dose 1 (on Day 15). At Dose 2, 16.3% of patients had an IRR.

Thereafter, the risk of IRRs decreased with subsequent infusions including their incidence and intensity. Overall, of the patients with at least one IRR during the DBP, 62.2% of patients experienced an IRR during infusion, and 48.9% experienced an IRR within 24 hours after the end of the infusion.

## Management of IRRs

IRRs were managed through slowing or interrupting the infusion, symptomatically with acetaminophen (paracetamol), intramuscular or slow IV antihistamine administration, or with other interventions as judged appropriate by the treating investigator.

Among patients in the OCR arm who experienced at least one IRR, 21/45 patients (46.7%) received treatment for IRR. Three patients experienced Grade 3 IRRs, of which, 2 patients received treatment for the IRR. For all 3 patients, the OCR infusion was interrupted and IRR symptoms subsequently resolved. Two of these patients resumed infusion on the same day, the third resumed treatment 37 days later and ultimately all 3 patients received the target dose.

## Premedication / Pre-Infusion Prophylactic Treatment

As required by the Protocol WN42086 v6, all patients (100.0%) received pre-infusion prophylactic treatment, including systemic corticosteroids and antihistamines during the study.

## OPERETTA 1

82.6% of patients (19/23) experienced a total of 61 IRRs in 60 infusions (out of a total of 240 infusions). Incidence of IRRs was similar between the two cohorts. All IRRs had resolved at the time of reporting, and no events resulted in dose delay/interruption or study treatment discontinuation. Headache and nasal dryness occurred twice each in Cohort 1; all other symptoms in Cohort 1 occurred once each. Common symptoms in Cohort 2 (occurring ≥ 5 times) were headache, throat irritation, rash, fatigue and myalgia.

## Cohort 1:

- 83.3% of patients (5/6) experienced a total of 8 IRRs during the study. All IRR events were non-serious and 6/8 events were NCI CTCAE Grade 1. All except one event had a duration of 1 or 2 days.
- One patient had two events at Grade 2:
- o One event consisting of symptoms of throat tightness, throat irritation and dyspnoea exertional lasted for one day and was treated;

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- o The other event (electrocardiogram ST segment abnormal) was not treated. An unscheduled ECG performed 30 days later was normal, whereby the AE was considered resolved.
- Three patients (50%) had IRRs during the first infusion.

## Cohort 2:

- 82.4% of patients (14/17) experienced a total of 53 IRRs during the study. All events were non-serious, and all, except for 3 Grade 3 events, were Grade 1 (27/53) or Grade 2 (23/53). There were no Grade 4 events.
- One patient (1/14; 7.1%) experienced a non-serious IRR with a most extreme intensity of Grade 3 during the first infusion. Symptoms associated with this IRR were dyspnoea, rhinorrhea, and urticaria. The patient was treated with diphenhydramine for this event, the infusion was temporarily interrupted then restarted, and the event resolved after 1 day.
- One patient experienced a non-serious IRR of Grade 3 on Day 1340, resolving the same day with treatment (famotidine, diphenhydramine and hydrocortisone), and no change to study drug. Symptoms of this IRR were cough, oropharyngeal pain, dyspnoea, rhinorrhea, rash and throat irritation. This same patient experienced a nonserious IRR of Grade 3 on Day 1698, without any treatment for AE and resolving after 6 days. This event occurred after the patient was withdrawn from study treatment during the OOE due to an AE, and had opted to receive treatment with commercial (non-study) ocrelizumab during the SFU. This event is not assessed by the investigator as related to study drug.

## Timing of IRRs

The highest incidence of IRRs was associated with Dose 1, Day 1 (56.5% [13/23]). Evaluating subsequent doses reveals variability in IRR frequency.

Among 19 patients with an IRR reported, 78.9% (15/19) experienced an IRR during the infusion, and 57.9% (11/19) experienced an IRR within 24 hours after the end of the infusion. 10 patients had a single IRR occurrence, the remaining 9 patients experienced recurrent IRRs. In the 9 patients with recurrent IRRs, the majority of IRRs were G1 or 2 (50/53 events); 48/53 events resolved within 1-4 days (the duration of 2 events was unknown; other events resolved in 6, 30 or 117 days). No action taken with study drug in all IRRs cases. Infusion-related reactions (IRRs) were more frequently observed in the U.S. (8/9) and Poland (6/7) than in Italy (5/7), suggesting a potential regional difference in their occurrence. Notably, among the 6 patients who experienced more than 5 recurrent IRRs, 5 patients were from U.S. and 1 patient was from Poland. All 6 patients received premedications prior to every dose. Potential risk factors for IRR (medical history of seasonal allergy) were identified for 3 patients.

As can be seen in Figure 6 in OPERETTA 1 CSR (CSR 1138959, Figure 6), especially in Cohort 1 of OPERETTA 1 the median B cell levels were starting to replete in between ocrelizumab doses. However, due to the small sample size in both cohorts of OPERETTA 1, these median B cell level increases are influenced by a small number of patients each time. A total of 4 patients showed B cell repletion; 1 patient had accompanying IRRs, while the other 3 patients with B-cell repletion between doses did not show IRR occurrences. Therefore, no evidence was observed to support a link between B-cell repletion and IRR occurrence in pediatric patients.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The recurrent IRRs in the 6 patients are more likely to have influenced the overall results than the variability of the B cell levels prior to infusion.

## Management of IRRs

IRRs were managed through slowing or interrupting the infusion and symptomatically with acetaminophen (paracetamol), IM or slow IV antihistamine administration, or with other interventions as judged appropriate by the treating investigator (diphenhydramine, famotidine and hydrocortisone).

## Premedication

All 23 patients (100.0%) received at least one preinfusion prophylactic treatment during the study. All patients received premedications in the following ATC Classes: antihistamines for systemic use, antipruritic including antihistamines and anaesthetics, corticosteroids for systemic use, and ophthalmological preparations.

## Hypersensitivity

AEs potentially representing hypersensitivity and anaphylactic reactions were retrieved using the MedDRA Anaphylactic reactions SMQ (Broad) and the Hypersensitivity SMQ (Broad). All AEs were subsequently medically reviewed for identification of an event representing a hypersensitivity or anaphylactic reaction related to study treatment.

## OPERETTA 2

There were no events of PT anaphylaxis or PT hypersensitivity. Following Sponsor medical review, it was determined there were no events indicative of hypersensitivity or anaphylactic reaction. Sponsor medical review of the 114 events in the OCR arm and 77 events in the FINGO arm retrieved by these two SMQs determined that the majority of the events were likely IRRs or respective symptoms of IRRs; two events were attributed to concomitant medications.

## OPERETTA 1

Following Sponsor medical review, it was determined there were no cases of hypersensitivity or anaphylactic reaction. Sponsor medical review of the 86 events reported in these two SMQs determined that the majority of the events were likely IRRs rather than true hypersensitivity reactions. None of the events were serious or acute, meeting the protocol requirement for permanent discontinuation of study drug. There were no events of PT anaphylaxis.

## Infections

## OPERETTA 2

This section summarizes infections as events coded to the MedDRA SOC of Infections and infestations.

The proportion of patients with at least one infection in the OCR arm (73.1%, with 204 events) was higher than the FINGO arm (58.7%, with 152 events), driven by differences in incidence of upper respiratory tract infection, nasopharyngitis, and influenza (See Table 14 for the most frequent infections by PT). The number of infections per 100PY, including COVID-19, was 149.17 (95% CI: 129.4, 171.1) in the OCR arm vs. 116.82 (95% CI: 98.99, 136.94) in the FINGO arm. The number of infections per 100PY, excluding COVID-19, was 146.2 (95% CI:126.7, 168.0) vs. 113.0 (95% CI: 95.5, 132.8).

Information on specific pathogen types for observed infections was not commonly available. Where pathogen type was known, the most common pathogen types were Viral not further specified and Bacterial not further specified.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Influenza

Since the imbalance in non-serious infections between the two treatment arms was highest for influenza, the Sponsor conducted a medical review with respect to patients' vaccination status and timing of influenza events. The following factors were considered:

The listing of AEs was filtered to identify patients experiencing AEs of influenza: 18 patients experienced AEs of influenza in the study

- 13 patients in the OCR arm, from Brazil (6 patients), Italy (2 patients) and one patient each from Belgium, Spain, France, Hungary, and U.S. All events were non-serious, Grade 1-2, and all AEs were resolved with no change to study drug (ocrelizumab).
- 5 patients in the FINGO arm, from Italy (2 patients) and one patient each from Brazil, Greece and Germany. One patient had a serious event, which resolved with no change to study drug (fingolimod). The other 4 events were Grade 1-2, and all AEs resolved. One event resulted in an interruption of fingolimod treatment.

In the OCR arm, there were three potential breakthrough cases, whereas no breakthrough cases were observed in the FINGO arm. These potential breakthrough infections were not serious, which suggests that the vaccine may have played a role in preventing severe outcomes. The observed imbalance in influenza cases is not attributed to breakthrough infections. It primarily originated from Brazil. The reasons for this imbalance remain unclear and were not observed or reproduced in any other country.

## Opportunistic Infections

No events of opportunistic infection were retrieved from the SMQ Opportunistic Infections (Narrow) for either treatment arm.

## COVID-19

The incidence of COVID-19 was similar in both treatment arms (4.3% of patients in each treatment arm: 4 events in the OCR arm and 5 events in the FINGO arm).

All events of COVID-19 infection were non-serious, Grade 1 or 2, and were resolved at the time of reporting and did not result in treatment interruption/discontinuation or study discontinuation.

## Serious Infections

The proportion of patients with at least one serious infection was balanced between the two treatment arms (3.2% vs. 3.3%; 4 events in each arm). The most frequent serious infection by PT was pneumonia (2.2% of patients with 3 events in the OCR arm and 1.1% with one event in the FINGO arm; Table 15). The number of serious infections per 100PY was 2.92 (95% CI: 0.8, 7.49) in the OCR arm and 3.07 (95% CI: 0.84, 7.87) in the FINGO arm.

The serious infections all followed the usual clinical course (Table 15). A case-level review (since a quantitative aggregate review was not possible due to low number of serious infections) found no association between serious infections and patients' immunoglobulin levels &lt; LLN in either treatment arm.

## OCR Arm

- One patient experienced Grade 3 pneumonia on Day 174 and Day 279, which resolved after 22 and 18 days, respectively. Pathogen was unknown. The patient received treatment for both events. Study drug was unchanged. All immunoglobulin levels were within normal range at the Week 22 visit, prior to the first pneumonia event on Day 174. However, at the Week 36 visit (Day 251, after the first event but before the second), IgA levels were higher than ULN and

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

IgM levels were below LLN (IgG remained normal at Week 36). The IgM levels remained below LLN at the Week 46 visit, which followed the second pneumonia event.

- One patient experienced Grade 3 peritonitis on Day 126, which resolved after 7 days. The patient received treatment for the event. The infectious nature was unknown. Placebo fingolimod was interrupted. The patient's immunoglobulin levels were within normal range at baseline and remained so at the Week 12 visit (Day 87) prior to the onset of peritonitis. The IgA, IgG and IgM levels also remained within the normal range at the subsequent Week 22 visit and up to the CCOD.
- Patient experienced Grade 3 pneumonia on Day 534, which resolved after 26 days. Mycoplasma pneumoniae test was positive. The patient received treatment for the event. The patient's immunoglobulin levels were normal at baseline. IgM levels were first observed to be below the normal range at the Week 22 visit (Day 148) and remained below LLN for the duration of the study. The IgA and IgG levels did not decrease below LLN.

## OPERETTA 1

All patients experienced at least one AE in this SOC, with 116 events reported. The rate of infections per 100PY was 139.6 (95% CI: 115.3, 167.4) (total PY at risk: 83.1 years). For serious infections, the rate was 8.4 (95% CI: 3.4, 17.4). COVID 19 events, which are included in the SOC of Infections and infestations, are further discussed in the COVID-19 subsection. All serious infections followed the usual clinical course.

Cohort 1: experienced 18 AEs in the SOC Infections and infestations

- There was one SAE of appendicitis reported by 1 patient
- All other reported PTs were non-serious, with a most extreme intensity of Grade 1 or 2
- The most frequent (≥ 30%) infections by PT were COVID-19, reported in 50.0% of patients (3/6); upper respiratory tract infection and influenza (reported in 33.3% patients each)
- All events were assessed by the investigator as not related to the study drug
- No events led to dose delay/interruption or discontinuation of study drug
- All events had resolved at the time of reporting; durations ranged from 1 to 31 days except for an event of a fungal infection lasting 634 days

Cohort 2: experienced 98 AEs in the SOC Infections and infestations.

- A total of 6 serious infections of Grade 3 were reported, all in 1 patient; 4 of them were assessed by the investigator as related to study drug.
- The majority of infections (91/98) reported in Cohort 2 were non-serious with a most extreme intensity of Grade 1 or 2.
- The most frequent (&gt; 30%) infections by PT were COVID-19 and upper respiratory tract infection (each reported in 64.7% [11/17 patients]), and nasopharyngitis (41.2% [7/17 patients].
- All AEs of infection, except for 2, had resolved within 1 to 54 days at the time of reporting.

## Opportunistic Infections

No events of opportunistic infection were retrieved from the SMQ Opportunistic Infections (Narrow) for either treatment arm.

## COVID-19

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

60.9% of patients (14/23) experienced non-serious, Grade 1 or 2, COVID-19 infection during the study. The impact of COVID-19 pandemic on safety was low.

## Neutropenia

## OPERETTA 2

Events were identified using the preferred terms 'Neutropenia', 'Febrile neutropenia' or 'Neutrophil count decreased'.

One patient in the OCR arm (1.1%) experienced one event, versus 10 patients in the FINGO arm experienced 18 events.

## OCR Arm

One patient experienced a non-serious Grade 2 AE of neutrophil count decreased on Day 1 of the study. The patient did not receive treatment for this event, which resolved after 8 days without change to study treatment. The AE of neutropenia also coincided with a laboratory abnormality of Grade L2 (low) total absolute neutrophil of 1.3x109/L on Day 1, confirming this patient had neutropenia at the study start.

## FINGO Arm

Overall, the neutropenia-related AEs were in line with the known safety profile of fingolimod (Gilenya (fingolimod) USPI; Gilenya (fingolimod) E.U. SmPC).

## OPERETTA 1

AEs of neutropenia were reported in 2 patients:

- One serious in one patient in Cohort 1.
- One further patient in Cohort 2 had three non-serious events of neutrophil count decreased not requiring treatment, all of which were assessed by the investigator as related to study drug. The Grade 1 and Grade 2 events led to study drug delay (reported as study drug interrupted) to satisfy protocol retreatment criteria, and resolved after 22 and 18 days, respectively. The Grade 3 event resolved after 187 days, with no change to study drug. None of the events were accompanied by infections or fever.

## Autoimmune Diseases

Autoimmune diseases were identified using the SMQ \"Immune-mediated/autoimmune disorders (Narrow)\".

## OPERETTA 2

The proportion of patients with events retrieved from the SMQ was similar in both treatment arms (3.2% vs. 3.3%). However, three of these events were coded to PT MS, the disease under study. The remaining events retrieved from this SMQ are as follows:

## OCR Arm

- One patient experienced a non-serious Grade 1 AE of psoriasis. The patient received treatment with Acitretin for the event, which resolved after 215 days. The patient had medical history of psoriasis before study treatment.
- One patient experienced a non-serious Grade 1 AE of butterfly rash. The patient did not receive treatment for the event, which resolved after 2 days.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## FINGO Arm

- One patient experienced a non-serious event of Grade 1 psoriasis. The patient received treatment with clobetasol propionate, betamethasone depropronate, betamethasone valerate, diflucortolone valerate, isoconazole nitrate, other antifungals for topical use for the event, which was ongoing at the time of the CCOD. The patient had medical history of hand dermatitis and fungal infection before study treatment.

## OPERETTA 1

There was one case of psoriasis in Cohort 2, which was non-serious, Grade 1, and assessed by the investigator as unrelated to study drug. The patient had medical history of autoimmune thyroiditis. The patient received treatment and study drug was not changed. The event had not resolved by the CCOD.

## Malignancies

No events of malignancy were retrieved from the MedDRA SMQ 'Malignant tumours (Narrow)' for either study.

## Laboratory findings

## Hematology

## OPERETTA 2

## Hematology Abnormalities in OPERETTA 2

Imbalances in marked abnormalities (greater than 10% difference between the two treatment arms) in hematological parameters between the OCR and FINGO arms were observed for: low levels of lymphocytes, absolute neutrophils, total leukocyte count, and erythrocytes, which occurred at a greater incidence in the FINGO arm than the OCR arm. The imbalance of hematological abnormalities could be largely attributed to the expected safety profile or mechanism of action of fingolimod (Gilenya (fingolimod) USPI; Gilenya (fingolimod) E.U. SmPC). However, in the OCR arm, there was a greater incidence of low levels of Erythrocyte Mean Corpuscular Volume and high erythrocytes than in the FINGO arm. The baseline imbalance between the 2 treatment arms could be a contributing factor. In the OCR arm, a higher proportion of patients had high Erythrocytes (3.23% vs. 0%). These imbalances in marked hematology abnormalities were not considered clinically relevant as they were not accompanied by any related AEs. No notable differences in marked abnormalities were observed between the OCR and FINGO arms in low hemoglobin, low hematocrit or low platelets.

## Adverse Events of Grade 3 or 4 Hematology Parameters in OPERETTA 2

## OCR Arm

No Grade 3 or 4 events were observed.

## FINGO Arm

- One patient had two events of non-serious Grade 3 leukopenia (resolving after 8 and 19 days) and three events of Grade 3 neutropenia (two serious, resolving after 47 and 13 days; one nonserious, resolving after 12 days). Fingolimod was interrupted for both cases of leukopenia and one case of neutropenia.
- Two patients with non-serious Grade 4 lymphocyte count decreased. Neither patient received treatment for the event. One event resulted in interruption of fingolimod; the event resolved after 31 days. The other event resolved after 58 days (no interruption to fingolimod).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Single instances in separate patients of non-serious Grade 3 events: white blood cell count decreased, resolving after 16 days; anaemia, unresolved at CCOD; leukopenia, resolving after 8 days. Fingolimod was not interrupted for any of these events.

## OPERETTA 1

## Hematology Abnormalities in OPERETTA 1

As of the CCOD, there were no clinically meaningful findings of laboratory abnormalities for hematology parameters in Cohort 1. Laboratory abnormalities were reported for the following parameters in Cohort 2:

- Single abnormalities of low lymphocytes in 17.6% of patients (3/17)
- Low total absolute neutrophils in 11.8% of patients (2/17) (one single abnormality, and one last/replicated abnormality)
- Single abnormalities of high total absolute neutrophils in 11.8% of patients (2/17)

No notable changes were seen in basophils, eosinophils, monocytes, platelets, hematocrit, hemoglobin, red blood cells, or total leukocyte count.

## Adverse Events of Grade 3 or 4 Hematology Parameters in OPERETTA 1

In Cohort 1, 1 patient experienced a serious Grade 4 event of neutropenia. In Cohort 2, one patient experienced one non-serious Grade 3 event of neutrophil count decreased, resolving after 187 days, with no change or delay to study treatment. This patient also experienced two non-serious Grade 1 and 2 events of neutrophil count decreased, which resulted in dose delay. All three events were assessed by the investigator as related to study drug, and all were resolved at the time of the CCOD.

## Chemistry

## OPERETTA 2

## Chemistry Abnormalities in OPERETTA 2

There were no patients with severe liver injury as defined by Hy's law in either treatment arm. One patient in the FINGO arm had elevated ALT at &gt; 3×ULN on two consecutive visits (Weeks 12 and 22) in combination with total bilirubin at ≤ 2×ULN.

Other than elevated calcium, where the difference was 15.6% vs. 4.5% (OCR vs. FINGO arms, respectively), there were no marked laboratory abnormalities occurring more frequently in the OCR arm and with more than 10% difference in the OCR arm than in the FINGO arm. These events of elevated calcium were not accompanied by AEs and were therefore not clinically relevant.

## Adverse Events of Grade 3 or 4 Chemistry Parameters in OPERETTA 2

OCR Arm:

- One patient had non-serious Grade 3 ALT increased. Placebo fingolimod was interrupted. The event resolved after 84 days.
- One patient had non-serious Grade 3 AST increased. Placebo fingolimod was interrupted. The event was resolving at CCOD.
- One patient had non-serious Grade 3 hepatic enzyme increased. Placebo fingolimod was interrupted. The event resolved after 43 days.

## FINGO Arm

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- One patient had serious Grade 3 transaminases increased. Treatment with fingolimod was withdrawn.
- One patient had non-serious Grade 3 ALT increased on two occasions. Treatment with fingolimod was interrupted for the first event; the event resolved after 72 days. Following the second event, fingolimod was withdrawn.

## OPERETTA 1

There were no patients with severe liver injury as defined by Hy's law in either cohorts. There were no new safety findings from chemistry analysis.

## Chemistry Abnormalities in OPERETTA 1

Laboratory abnormalities in serum chemistry were reported for the following parameters:
